## P2 - Data race on the `errs` slice in `CLab.Save`

**Location:** [core/save.go:41-62](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/core/save.go#L41-L62)

**Problem:** The per-node goroutines launched in `CLab.Save` capture the outer `errs` variable by reference and execute `errs[i] = ...` while the main goroutine continues to run `errs = append(errs, nil)`. There is no happens-before edge between a goroutine&#39;s access and the main loop&#39;s later appends, so the goroutines and the main loop race on the slice header. The race is value-benign today only because `errs` is created with `make([]error, 0, len(c.Nodes))`, so `append` never reallocates the backing array; that invariant is implicit and fragile, and `go test -race` &#40;including the new `TestSaveReturnsNodeErrors`, which spawns three such goroutines&#41; flags it whenever race mode is enabled.

**Trigger:** Any `clab save` on the Docker runtime path with two or more nodes; detectable by running `go test -race ./core/`, which the CI workflows currently do not do.

**Impact:** Undefined behavior under the Go memory model. No lost writes occur today because the backing array is never reallocated, but any future change to the capacity expression reintroduces the risk of silently dropping node `save` errors, and race-detector builds will report the race.

**Suggested correction:** Preallocate by length and assign by index&#58; `errs := make([]error, len(c.Nodes))` with each goroutine writing `errs[i]` for its pre-assigned index and no appends anywhere. This matches the existing in-repo pattern in [`labruntime/clabernetes/workers.go:45`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/labruntime/clabernetes/workers.go#L45-L45) &#40;`errs := make([]error, len(items))`&#41; and also removes the awkward `errs = append(errs, nil); i := len(errs)-1` idiom. Note that `i` is already a per-iteration local, so there is no loop-variable capture issue; the defect is solely the concurrent slice-header access.


## P4 - `EndpointsWithParked` returns the live `d.Endpoints` slice with no usage contract

**Location:** [nodes/default&#95;node.go:1115-1127](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/nodes/default_node.go#L1115-L1127)

**Problem:** When `d.parkingNode` is nil, which is the steady state for every normal deploy, `EndpointsWithParked` returns `d.Endpoints` itself, sharing the node&#39;s backing array with the caller. The parked branch deliberately clones &#40;`slices.Clone`, asserted by `TestDefaultNodeEndpointsWithParked`&#41;, but the common case has no such protection and the method has no doc comment stating the contract.

**Trigger:** A future caller that appends to or mutates the returned slice, for example appending an endpoint before `AdoptEndpoint`, which grows the shared backing array and can corrupt node state.

**Impact:** No current caller mutates the result; all four call sites only read it. This is a latent API hazard, not an active defect.

**Suggested correction:** Add a short doc comment to `EndpointsWithParked` stating that the returned slice must not be mutated, mirroring the aliasing guarantee the parked branch already provides. Optionally always `slices.Clone`, but a comment is the proportionate fix.


## P4 - No check covers the newly propagated `copySavedConfig` errors in `Save`

**Location:** [core/save&#95;test.go:14-75](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/core/save_test.go#L14-L75)

**Problem:** This PR changes two error paths in `Save`&#58; `SaveConfig` failures &#40;covered by the new `TestSaveReturnsNodeErrors`&#41; and `copySavedConfig` failures, which the base version only logged and which are now returned to the caller. The new test exercises only the first path, so the second changed contract is untested.

**Trigger:** Running `clab save --copy-out <dst>` where the copy fails, for example when `node.Config()` returns nil, producing the `node config missing` error from `copySavedConfig`.

**Impact:** A regression that drops or mis-reports copy failures would go unnoticed. Risk is low because `copySavedConfig` itself is unchanged, but the propagation change is exactly what the PR introduces.

**Suggested correction:** Add one table case to `TestSaveReturnsNodeErrors` &#40;or a sibling test&#41; that calls `c.Save(context.Background(), WithCopyOut(t.TempDir()))` with a mock whose `SaveConfig` returns a populated `*nodes.SaveConfigResult` and whose `Config()` returns nil, then asserts the joined error contains `node config missing`. This is roughly a ten-line addition using the existing mock.


## Coverage

- Source-only review&#58; no code was executed, so no tests were run and no race or formatting failures were observed directly; compilation of the new tests was checked statically against existing symbols &#40;`links.NewEndpointGeneric`, `links.NewEndpointVeth`, `Node.AdoptEndpoint`/`ReleaseEndpoint`, `links.NewParkingNode`, `mocknodes.NewMockNode`&#41;.
- All nine changed paths were inspected with their base versions&#58; [`core/save.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/core/save.go#L1-L1), [`core/save_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/core/save_test.go#L1-L1), [`docs/cmd/save.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/docs/cmd/save.md#L1-L1), [`docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md#L1-L1), [`nodes/default_node.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/nodes/default_node.go#L1-L1), [`nodes/default_node_test.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/nodes/default_node_test.go#L1-L1), [`nodes/iol/iol.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/nodes/iol/iol.go#L1-L1), [`nodes/linux/linux.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/nodes/linux/linux.go#L1-L1), [`nodes/xrd/xrd.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/nodes/xrd/xrd.go#L1-L1).
- The park -&#62; recreate -&#62; restore flow was traced through [`core/lifecycle.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/core/lifecycle.go#L1-L1), [`core/apply.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/core/apply.go#L1-L1), [`links/parking_node.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/links/parking_node.go#L1-L1), and [`links/endpoint.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/links/endpoint.go#L1-L1) &#40;`moveEndpoint` releases from the source node before adopting into the parking node&#41;, confirming parked-endpoint counting in `EndpointsWithParked` is correct and free of double counting, including the failed-restore path where `d.parkingNode` is retained.
- Only node kinds that set `CLAB_INTFS` were updated &#40;`linux`, `default_node` consumers `iol` and `xrd`&#41;; a grep for `ClabEnvIntfs` confirms no other call sites exist, and `EndpointsWithParked` is only needed on the concrete `DefaultNode` type, not the `nodes.Node` interface.
- CI workflows run golangci-lint and clabernetes core tests but no Go tests with `-race`, which is why the P2 race is unlikely to surface in current CI; the finding stands on memory-model grounds.
- The image-level claims in [`docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md#L1-L1) &#40;Telnet on port 5000, availability only in `srl-labs/vrnetlab` images built after November 2026&#41; describe external `vrnetlab` behavior and cannot be verified from this repository; the tab/admonition nesting was checked structurally and is valid.
- The release-note rule &#40;[`.cursor/rules/release-note-review.mdc`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/.cursor/rules/release-note-review.mdc#L1-L1)&#41; was checked&#58; no files under docs/rn/ are touched, so it raises nothing; whether the `save` exit-code change warrants a release note is a maintainer decision.
- No topology schema changes are involved, so [`schemas/clab.schema.json`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/schemas/clab.schema.json#L1-L1) was not consulted beyond confirming the PR does not touch it.
- [`docs/cmd/save.md`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/docs/cmd/save.md#L1-L1) files are hand-written prose &#40;not generated&#41;, and the new non-zero-exit claim matches [`cmd/save.go`](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/0581db29d40ff5d50b64dce371fb0c0f500406e5/cmd/save.go#L1-L1) returning `c.Save(...)` from `RunE` as well as error propagation through `saveWithLabRuntime` for the clabernetes runtime path.

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

78: +)
79: +
80: +func TestSaveReturnsNodeErrors(t *testing.T) {
81: +	errBoom := errors.New("boom")
82: +
83: +	tests := []struct {
84: +		name    string
85: +		failing map[string]bool
86: +		wantErr []string // substrings the joined error must contain; nil means no error
87: +	}{
88: +		{name: "all nodes save", failing: nil},
89: +		{
90: +			name:    "one node fails",
91: +			failing: map[string]bool{"r2": true},
92: +			wantErr: []string{`node "r2" save failed: boom`},
93: +		},
94: +		{
95: +			name:    "every node fails",
96: +			failing: map[string]bool{"r1": true, "r2": true, "r3": true},
97: +			wantErr: []string{
98: +				`node "r1" save failed: boom`,
99: +				`node "r2" save failed: boom`,
100: +				`node "r3" save failed: boom`,
101: +			},
102: +		},
103: +	}
104: +
105: +	for _, tt := range tests {
106: +		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
107: +			ctrl := gomock.NewController(t)
108: +			c := &CLab{Nodes: map[string]clabnodes.Node{}}
109: +			for _, name := range []string{"r1", "r2", "r3"} {
110: +				n := clabmocksmocknodes.NewMockNode(ctrl)
111: +				n.EXPECT().GetShortName().Return(name).AnyTimes()
112: +				var err error
113: +				if tt.failing[name] {
114: +					err = errBoom
115: +				}
116: +				n.EXPECT().SaveConfig(gomock.Any()).Return(nil, err)
117: +				c.Nodes[name] = n
118: +			}
119: +
120: +			err := c.Save(context.Background())
121: +
122: +			if tt.wantErr == nil {
123: +				if err != nil {
124: +					t.Fatalf("got error %v, want nil", err)
125: +				}
126: +				return
127: +			}
128: +			if !errors.Is(err, errBoom) {
129: +				t.Fatalf("got error %v, want it to wrap %v", err, errBoom)
130: +			}
131: +			for _, want := range tt.wantErr {
132: +				if !strings.Contains(err.Error(), want) {
133: +					t.Errorf("error %q does not contain %q", err, want)
134: +				}
135: +			}
136: +			if got := strings.Count(err.Error(), "save failed"); got != len(tt.wantErr) {
137: +				t.Errorf("error reports %d failures, want %d: %q", got, len(tt.wantErr), err)
138: +			}
139: +		})
140: +	}
141: +}

148:  ### Description
149:  
150:  The `save` command perform configuration save for all the containers running in a lab.
151:  
152: +When `save` fails, it will exit with a non-zero return code and reports each node that failed configuration save.
153: +
154:  /// note | Clabernetes runtime
155:  With `--runtime c9s`, `save` runs the kind-specific save command in each device
156:  container. `save --copy` streams the resulting files back to the local
157:  destination using the same copy layout as Docker labs. See
158:  [Containerlab runtime](../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#save) for details.

257: +func TestDefaultNodeEndpointsWithParked(t *testing.T) {
258: +	newNode := func() *DefaultNode {
259: +		return &DefaultNode{
260: +			Cfg: &clabtypes.NodeConfig{ShortName: "node1", LongName: "clab-test-node1"},
261: +		}
262: +	}
263: +	veth := func(n clablinks.Node, iface string) clablinks.Endpoint {
264: +		return clablinks.NewEndpointVeth(clablinks.NewEndpointGeneric(n, iface, nil))
265: +	}
266: +
267: +	tests := []struct {
268: +		name   string
269: +		owned  []string
270: +		parked []string // nil means no parking node
271: +		want   []string
272: +	}{
273: +		{name: "no parking node", owned: []string{"eth1", "eth2"}, want: []string{"eth1", "eth2"}},
274: +		{
275: +			name:   "all parked for recreate",
276: +			owned:  nil,
277: +			parked: []string{"eth1", "eth2"},
278: +			want:   []string{"eth1", "eth2"},
279: +		},
280: +		{
281: +			name:   "owned and parked split after a failed restore",
282: +			owned:  []string{"eth3"},
283: +			parked: []string{"eth1", "eth2"},
284: +			want:   []string{"eth3", "eth1", "eth2"},
285: +		},
286: +	}
287: +	for _, tt := range tests {
288: +		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
289: +			d := newNode()
290: +			for _, iface := range tt.owned {
291: +				if err := d.AdoptEndpoint(veth(d, iface)); err != nil {
292: +					t.Fatalf("adopt %s: %v", iface, err)
293: +				}
294: +			}
295: +			if tt.parked != nil {
296: +				d.parkingNode = clablinks.NewParkingNode(d.Cfg.LongName, "/run/netns/test")
297: +				for _, iface := range tt.parked {
298: +					if err := d.parkingNode.AddEndpoint(veth(d.parkingNode, iface)); err != nil {
299: +						t.Fatalf("park %s: %v", iface, err)
300: +					}
301: +				}
302: +			}
303: +
304: +			// Spare capacity lets a missing clone show up as aliasing.
305: +			d.Endpoints = slices.Grow(d.Endpoints, 4)
306: +
307: +			got := d.EndpointsWithParked()
308: +			var names []string
309: +			for _, ep := range got {
310: +				names = append(names, ep.GetIfaceName())
311: +			}
312: +			if !slices.Equal(names, tt.want) {
313: +				t.Fatalf("got %v, want %v", names, tt.want)
314: +			}
315: +
316: +			// The result must not alias d.Endpoints when parked endpoints are added,
317: +			// or appending to it would corrupt the node's own list.
318: +			if tt.parked != nil && len(d.Endpoints) > 0 && &got[0] == &d.Endpoints[0] {
319: +				t.Fatal("result aliases d.Endpoints")
320: +			}
321: +			if len(d.Endpoints) != len(tt.owned) {
322: +				t.Fatalf("d.Endpoints changed: got %d, want %d", len(d.Endpoints), len(tt.owned))
323: +			}
324: +		})
325: +	}
326: +}

21: 	options ...SaveOption,

24: 	for _, opt := range options {
25: 		opt(opts)

72: func (c *CLab) resolveCopyOutDst(dst string) (string, error) {
73: 	baseDir, err := os.Getwd()
74: 	if err != nil || baseDir == "" {
75: 		baseDir = c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir()
76: 	}
77: 	if baseDir == "" {
78: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve save dst: current working directory is empty")
79: 	}

84: 	if labDirName == "" || labDirName == "." {

100: // copySavedConfig copies the saved configuration file from the path in the SaveConfigResult
101: // to the user-specified destination directory with a timestamp in the filename.
102: // A symlink with the original filename is created (or updated) to point to the
103: // latest timestamped copy.
104: //
105: // The destination layout is:
106: //
107: //	<dstRoot>/<nodeName>/config-060102_150405.json   # timestamped copy
108: //	<dstRoot>/<nodeName>/config.json                 # symlink -> timestamped copy
109: func (c *CLab) copySavedConfig(
110: 	ctx context.Context,
111: 	result *clabnodes.SaveConfigResult,
112: 	node clabnodes.Node,
113: 	dstRoot string,
114: ) error {
115: 	if result == nil || result.ConfigPath == "" {
116: 		log.Debug("no saved config path reported, skipping copy", "node", node.GetShortName())
117: 		return nil
118: 	}
119: 
120: 	nodeCfg := node.Config()
121: 	if nodeCfg == nil {
122: 		return fmt.Errorf("node config missing")
123: 	}
124: 
125: 	nodeDstDir := filepath.Join(dstRoot, nodeCfg.ShortName)
126: 	if err := os.MkdirAll(nodeDstDir, clabconstants.PermissionsDirDefault); err != nil {
127: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create save dst node dir %q: %w", nodeDstDir, err)
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	fileName := filepath.Base(result.ConfigPath)
131: 	tsFileName := timestampedFileName(fileName, time.Now())
132: 
133: 	tsPath := filepath.Join(nodeDstDir, tsFileName)
134: 	if err := clabutils.CopyFile(ctx, result.ConfigPath, tsPath,
135: 		clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault); err != nil {
136: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to copy saved config from %q to %q: %w",
137: 			result.ConfigPath, tsPath, err)
138: 	}
139: 
140: 	// Create or update a symlink with the original filename pointing to the
141: 	// timestamped copy. Remove any existing file/symlink first.
142: 	linkPath := filepath.Join(nodeDstDir, fileName)
143: 	_ = os.Remove(linkPath)
144: 	if err := os.Symlink(tsFileName, linkPath); err != nil {
145: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create symlink %q -> %q: %w", linkPath, tsFileName, err)
146: 	}
147: 
148: 	log.Info(
149: 		"copied saved config",
150: 		"node", nodeCfg.ShortName,
151: 		"src", result.ConfigPath,
152: 		"dst", tsPath,
153: 	)
154: 
155: 	return nil
156: }
157: 
158: // timestampedFileName inserts a timestamp before the file extension.
159: // e.g. "config.json" with ts 2026-02-06 23:59:00 UTC -> "config-260206_235900.json".
160: // Files without an extension get the timestamp appended: "startup-config" ->
161: // "startup-config-260206_235900".
162: func timestampedFileName(name string, t time.Time) string {
163: 	ts := t.UTC().Format("060102_150405")
164: 	ext := filepath.Ext(name)
165: 	base := strings.TrimSuffix(name, ext)
166: 
167: 	return base + "-" + ts + ext
168: }

14: func TestSaveReturnsNodeErrors(t *testing.T) {
15: 	errBoom := errors.New("boom")
16: 
17: 	tests := []struct {
18: 		name    string
19: 		failing map[string]bool
20: 		wantErr []string // substrings the joined error must contain; nil means no error

24: 			name:    "one node fails",

40: 		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
41: 			ctrl := gomock.NewController(t)
42: 			c := &CLab{Nodes: map[string]clabnodes.Node{}}
43: 			for _, name := range []string{"r1", "r2", "r3"} {

45: 				n.EXPECT().GetShortName().Return(name).AnyTimes()
46: 				var err error
47: 				if tt.failing[name] {
48: 					err = errBoom
49: 				}

51: 				c.Nodes[name] = n
52: 			}
53:

55: 
56: 			if tt.wantErr == nil {
57: 				if err != nil {
58: 					t.Fatalf("got error %v, want nil", err)
59: 				}
60: 				return
61: 			}
62: 			if !errors.Is(err, errBoom) {
63: 				t.Fatalf("got error %v, want it to wrap %v", err, errBoom)
64: 			}
65: 			for _, want := range tt.wantErr {
66: 				if !strings.Contains(err.Error(), want) {
67: 					t.Errorf("error %q does not contain %q", err, want)
68: 				}
69: 			}

71: 				t.Errorf("error reports %d failures, want %d: %q", got, len(tt.wantErr), err)
72: 			}
73: 		})

Reasoning
Now, important: is the data race real? Let me re-examine. The goroutine closure: `go func(node clabnodes.Node) { ... errs[i] = ... }`. `i` is a per-iteration local variable, fine. `errs` is the outer variable — captured by reference. Goroutine writes `errs[i]` concurrently while main continues `errs = append(errs, nil)`. Data race on the slice header variable `errs`.

Hmm wait — actually, is that true in Go semantics? The closure captures `errs` by reference (it's assigned in outer scope after closure creation). Yes, the closure body references the variable `errs`, so it reads its current value. Concurrent read (goroutine) and write (main append) of the same variable without synchronization = data race per Go memory model. Race detector would flag it under -race.

But note: does the goroutine even touch errs in the success path? No. So the race requires a failing node whose goroutine runs while the loop is still spawning/appending. SaveConfig typically takes time (exec into container), so a fast-failing early node plus many nodes → overlap is quite plausible. Also for small labs, the loop finishes quickly, but with goroutine scheduling there is still a window. Race detector would catch it nondeterministically in tests — the new save_test.go uses mocks; `SaveConfig` mock returns immediately, so the goroutine might complete before the next append... but the race detector only needs overlap; with immediate mock returns, the goroutine for r1 likely completes before main appends r2. Actually, main spawns goroutine and immediately continues; goroutine needs to be scheduled. Possible overlap. Anyway, it's a genuine data race per spec.

Better fix: preallocate `errs := make([]error, len(c.Nodes))` and iterate with an index counter — that removes both the append-during-race and any header access. Since `c.Nodes` is a map, iterate `for _, node := range c.Nodes { errs[i] = ...; go func(i int, node) }`. This is the classic pattern.

Severity: P2 (correctness/reliability defect — data race; could theoretically corrupt errors, and CI with -race may flag). Actually is there a `go test -race` in CI? Let me check cicid.yml quickly. Not strictly necessary.

Now check `parkingNode` lifecycle in default_node.go around lines 1180-1242, to see whether EndpointsWithParked semantics are right. Also check `GetEndpoints` callers to see if any other spot should count parked (e.g., `verifyIfHostEndpointsExist`, `Deploy` etc.). Let me read default_node.go sections.

Also check iol.go: `iolPortForLinuxIdx` and whether `ensureNumSlotsEnv` is called before endpoints parked... Also whether `n.EndpointsWithParked()` in iol is correct: iol embeds DefaultNode? Check.

Also xrd: `genInterfacesEnv` — check it's called at Deploy time.

Also check the docs: `clab save` cmd error propagation. Find cmd/root or cmd save.go.

Also the cisco_iol docs: "after November 2026" — the PR claims console requires image built after November 2026; today is Nov 2026. Just a doc claim, unverifiable. But maybe check that docs tab markup is correct (the `///` structure). Let me read the doc section.

Also check whether there's a schema concern: EndpointsWithParked is internal; no schema change needed.

Reasoning
Note `EndpointsWithParked` returns `d.Endpoints` directly when no parkingNode (not a clone) — the caller only reads len or iterates, so that's fine, and the test verifies no aliasing only when parked != nil. Actually when parked != nil, `slices.Clone` gives fresh backing array — good.

Now, when is `Deploy` called with parked endpoints? Deploy is called during `clab deploy`/`redeploy`. If a node was previously deployed and its endpoints were parked (e.g., after failed restore), on redeploy the container is recreated and CLAB_INTFS must count parked endpoints too since the container will have those interfaces once restored... Actually wait — when the container is recreated, the old interfaces are parked in the parking netns (they were moved out of the old container). On redeploy, new container is created; parked interfaces belong to parking netns and will be moved into the new container. Hmm, but actually for vrnetlab, CLAB_INTFS is used to postpone startup until all interfaces are present. So counting parked endpoints makes the VM wait for them. Correct.

But wait — one subtlety: does Deploy get called when there are parked endpoints in normal `clab deploy` after `clab destroy`? No — destroy removes everything.

Now, is `EndpointsWithParked` a method only on DefaultNode (not on the Node interface)? iol, linux, xrd embed DefaultNode, so they can call it directly. Check the embeds. Also check whether other nodes that set CLAB_INTFS via DefaultNode.Deploy include vrnetlab-based nodes — DefaultNode.Deploy is the generic path used by all nodes that don't override Deploy. Are there other overrides that set CLAB_INTFS? grep showed only linux and default_node. OK.

Are there other places that compute interface counts that should include parked? E.g., in `Deploy` for other node kinds (ceos? srl?). Let me grep for `GetEndpoints()` usages in nodes to see if any other count-based logic exists. Actually let me check `iol.go` fully around relevant sections, xrd.go, linux.go.

Also check iol: `iolPortForLinuxIdx` and mgmtSlot; `ensureNumSlotsEnv` called in Deploy or PreDeploy? If parked endpoints included, slots needed increase — right.

Also: does iol's `GenInterfaceConfig` handle parked endpoints correctly? Parked endpoints have interface names; generating interface config for them is needed since they'll be restored into the container. Yes.

Now check whether `EndpointsWithParked` should be added to the `clabnodes.Node` interface — not needed since only concrete types use it.

Check the cmd save propagation: find cmd save.go in head. Let me grep for `Save(` in cmd.

74: func (n *linux) Deploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.DeployParams) error {
75: 	// Set the "CLAB_INTFS" variable to the number of interfaces
76: 	// Which is required by vrnetlab to determine if all configured interfaces are present

81: 	if err != nil {
82: 		return err

99: // Start re-applies the eth0 TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime recreates
100: // the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
101: // See disableEth0TxOffload for why TX offload must stay disabled on eth0.
102: func (n *linux) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
103: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
104: 		return err
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix
108: 	// for them as well, as there is no container netns to adjust.

114: 
115: 	return nil

118: // disableEth0TxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the container's eth0 interface.
119: // Frames crossing the veth with an offloaded (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum break consumers
120: // that bridge or capture traffic on that interface, so the setting is (re-)applied on
121: // deploy and after every stop/start cycle, when the runtime recreates the mgmt veth.
122: //
123: // Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,
124: // and toggling its offload settings would alter the host networking state.
125: func (n *linux) disableEth0TxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
126: 	if n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "host" {
127: 		return
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.NSEthtoolTXOff(n.GetShortName(), "eth0"))
131: 	if err != nil {
132: 		log.Error(err)
133: 	}
134: }

139: 
140: 	return images

143: // CheckInterfaceName allows any interface name for linux nodes, but checks
144: // if eth0 is only used with network-mode=none.

200: }
201: 
202: // swapMgmtIdx swaps linux interface index 0 with the management interface's index,
203: // so Ethernet0/0 takes the index a relocated management interface vacated.
204: func (n *iol) swapMgmtIdx(idx int) int {
205: 	switch idx {
206: 	case 0:
207: 		return n.mgmtLinuxIdx
208: 	case n.mgmtLinuxIdx:
209: 		return 0
210: 	}
211: 
212: 	return idx
213: }
214: 
215: // iolPortForLinuxIdx returns the IOL slot/port backed by linux interface index idx.
216: func (n *iol) iolPortForLinuxIdx(idx int) (slot, port int) {
217: 	mapped := n.swapMgmtIdx(idx)
218: 	return mapped / 4, mapped % 4
219: }
220: 
221: // ensureNumSlotsEnv exports CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS when the management interface is
222: // relocated, so that its slot exists even when no link lands in it.
223: func (n *iol) ensureNumSlotsEnv() {
224: 	if n.mgmtLinuxIdx == 0 || n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] != "" {
225: 		return
226: 	}
227: 
228: 	intfIdxRegexp := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
229: 	needed := n.mgmtSlot + 1
230: 
231: 	for _, ep := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {
232: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(intfIdxRegexp.FindString(ep.GetIfaceName()))
233: 		if slot, _ := n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x); slot+1 > needed {
234: 			needed = slot + 1
235: 		}
236: 	}
237: 
238: 	n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] = strconv.Itoa(needed)
239: }
240: 
241: func (n *iol) PreDeploy(ctx context.Context, params *clabnodes.PreDeployParams) error {
242: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(n.Cfg.LabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)
243: 
244: 	if v := n.kindSpecificCfg().BootstrapConfig; v != "" {
245: 		switch p := clabutils.ResolvePath(v, params.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir()); {
246: 		case strings.EqualFold(v, "none"):
247: 			n.bootstrapNone = true
248: 		case clabutils.FileExists(p):
249: 			n.bootstrapCfgFile = p
250: 		default:
251: 			return fmt.Errorf("bootstrap-config file %q does not exist", p)
252: 		}
253: 	}
254: 
255: 	_, err := n.LoadOrGenerateCertificate(params.Cert, params.TopologyName)
256: 	if err != nil {
257: 		return err
258: 	}
259: 
260: 	n.ensureNumSlotsEnv()
261: 
262: 	return n.CreateIOLFiles(ctx)
263: }
264: 
265: func (n *iol) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
266: 	log.Infof("Running postdeploy actions for Cisco IOL '%s' node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
267: 
268: 	n.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)
269: 
270: 	if err := n.GenBootConfig(ctx); err != nil {
271: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to generate boot config: %w", err)
272: 	}
273: 
274: 	// Must update mgmt IP if not first boot, unless the node boots without a baseline config
275: 	if !n.firstBoot && !n.bootstrapNone {
276: 		return n.UpdateMgmtIntf(ctx)
277: 	}
278: 
279: 	return nil
280: }
281: 
282: // Start re-applies the mgmt veth TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime
283: // recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
284: // See disableMgmtTxOffload for why IOL cannot function with it enabled.
285: func (n *iol) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
286: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
287: 		return err
288: 	}
289: 
290: 	// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix
291: 	// for them as well, as there is no container mgmt veth to adjust.
292: 	if n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
293: 		return nil
294: 	}
295: 
296: 	n.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)
297: 
298: 	return nil
299: }
300: 
301: // disableMgmtTxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the host NS veth for the mgmt interface.
302: // iouyap bridges IOL interfaces over packet sockets; frames carrying an offloaded
303: // (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum reach the IOL process with an incomplete checksum, which its
304: // TCP/IP stack drops, breaking TCP/UDP connectivity.
305: //
306: // Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,
307: // and there is no clab-created mgmt veth to adjust.
308: func (n *iol) disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
309: 	if n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "host" {
310: 		return
311: 	}
312: 
313: 	if n.Runtime.Mgmt().Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {
314: 		return
315: 	}
316: 
317: 	var peerIfIndex int
318: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.VethPeerIndex("eth0", &peerIfIndex))
319: 	if err != nil {
320: 		log.Warn("Failed to get veth peer index for IOL mgmt interface",
321: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
322: 			"error", err)
323: 		return
324: 	}
325: 
326: 	if err := clabutils.DisableTxOffloadByIndex(peerIfIndex); err != nil {
327: 		log.Warn("Failed to disable TX checksum offload on IOL mgmt host veth",
328: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
329: 			"error", err)
330: 	}
331: }
332: 
333: func (n *iol) CreateIOLFiles(ctx context.Context) error {
334: 	// If NVRAM already exists, don't need to create
335: 	// otherwise saved configs in NVRAM are overwritten.
336: 	if !clabutils.FileExists(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile)) {
337: 		// create nvram file
338: 		clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile), "")
339: 		n.firstBoot = true
340: 	}
341: 	// create these files so the bind monut doesn't automatically
342: 	// make folders.
343: 	if bootCfg := path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "boot_config.txt"); !clabutils.FileExists(bootCfg) {
344: 		clabutils.CreateFile(bootCfg, "")
345: 	}
346: 
347: 	return n.GenInterfaceConfig(ctx)
348: }
349: 
350: // Generate interfaces configuration for IOL (and iouyap/netmap).
351: func (n *iol) GenInterfaceConfig(_ context.Context) error {
352: 	// add 'boilerplate' to NETMAP and iouyap.ini for the management port
353: 	iouyapData := fmt.Sprintf(
354: 		"[default]\nbase_port = 49000\nnetmap = /iol/NETMAP\n[513:%d/%d]\neth_dev = eth0\n",
355: 		n.mgmtSlot,
356: 		n.mgmtPort,
357: 	)
358: 	netmapdata := fmt.Sprintf(
359: 		"%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\n",
360: 		n.Pid,
361: 		n.mgmtSlot,
362: 		n.mgmtPort,
363: 		n.mgmtSlot,
364: 		n.mgmtPort,
365: 	)
366: 
367: 	slot, port := 0, 0
368: 
369: 	// Regexp to pull number out of linux'ethX' interface naming
370: 	IntfRegExpr := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
371: 
372: 	for _, intf := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {
373: 		// get numeric interface number and cast to int
374: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(IntfRegExpr.FindString(intf.GetIfaceName()))
375: 
376: 		// Interface naming is Ethernet{slot}/{port}. Each slot contains max 4 ports
377: 		slot, port = n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x)
378: 
379: 		// append data to write to NETMAP and IOUYAP files

23: 	clabutils "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/utils"
24: )
25: 
26: var (
27: 	kindNames          = []string{"xrd", "cisco_xrd"}
28: 	defaultCredentials = clabnodes.NewCredentials("clab", "clab@123")
29: 	xrdEnv             = map[string]string{
30: 		"XR_FIRST_BOOT_CONFIG": "/etc/xrd/first-boot.cfg",
31: 		"XR_MGMT_INTERFACES":   "linux:eth0,xr_name=Mg0/RP0/CPU0/0,chksum,snoop_v4,snoop_v6",
32: 		"XR_EVERY_BOOT_SCRIPT": "/etc/xrd/mgmt_intf_v6_addr.sh",
33: 	}
34: 
35: 	//go:embed mgmt_intf_v6_addr.sh.tmpl
36: 	scriptTemplate string
37: 
38: 	xrdMgmtScriptTpl, _ = template.New("clab-xrd-mgmt-ipv6-script").Funcs(
39: 		clabutils.CreateFuncs()).Parse(scriptTemplate)
40: 
41: 	//go:embed xrd.cfg
42: 	cfgTemplate string
43: )
44: 
45: const (
46: 	generateable     = true
47: 	generateIfFormat = "eth%d"
48: 
49: 	scrapliPlatformName = "cisco_iosxr"
50: 	NapalmPlatformName  = "iosxr"
51: )
52: 
53: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
54: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
55: 	generateNodeAttributes := clabnodes.NewGenerateNodeAttributes(generateable, generateIfFormat)
56: 	platformAttrs := &clabnodes.PlatformAttrs{
57: 		ScrapliPlatformName: scrapliPlatformName,
58: 		NapalmPlatformName:  NapalmPlatformName,
59: 	}
60: 
61: 	nrea := clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(
62: 		defaultCredentials,
63: 		generateNodeAttributes,
64: 		platformAttrs,
65: 	)
66: 
67: 	r.Register(kindNames, func() clabnodes.Node {
68: 		return new(xrd)
69: 	}, nrea)
70: }
71: 
72: type xrd struct {
73: 	clabnodes.DefaultNode
74: }
75: 
76: func (n *xrd) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
77: 	// Init DefaultNode
78: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)
79: 
80: 	n.Cfg = cfg
81: 	for _, o := range opts {
82: 		o(n)
83: 	}
84: 
85: 	n.Cfg.Binds = append(
86: 		n.Cfg.Binds,
87: 		// mount first-boot config file
88: 		fmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "first-boot.cfg"), ":/etc/xrd/first-boot.cfg"),
89: 		// persist data by mounting /xr-storage
90: 		fmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "xr-storage"), ":/xr-storage"),
91: 		// management IPv6 address script
92: 		fmt.Sprint(
93: 			filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "mgmt_intf_v6_addr.sh"),
94: 			":/etc/xrd/mgmt_intf_v6_addr.sh",
95: 		),
96: 	)
97: 
98: 	return nil
99: }
100: 
101: func (n *xrd) PreDeploy(ctx context.Context, params *clabnodes.PreDeployParams) error {
102: 	n.genInterfacesEnv()
103: 
104: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(n.Cfg.LabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)
105: 
106: 	_, err := n.LoadOrGenerateCertificate(params.Cert, params.TopologyName)
107: 	if err != nil {
108: 		return err
109: 	}
110: 
111: 	return n.createXRDFiles(ctx)
112: }
113: 
114: func (n *xrd) PostDeploy(_ context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
115: 	log.Infof("Running postdeploy actions for Cisco XRd '%s' node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
116: 
117: 	// create interface script template
118: 	tpl := xrdScriptTmpl{
119: 		MgmtIPv6Addr:      n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Address,
120: 		MgmtIPv6PrefixLen: n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6PrefixLength,
121: 	}
122: 
123: 	// create script from template
124: 	buf := new(bytes.Buffer)
125: 	err := xrdMgmtScriptTpl.Execute(buf, tpl)
126: 	if err != nil {
127: 		return err
128: 	}
129: 	// write it to disk
130: 	clabutils.CreateFile(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "mgmt_intf_v6_addr.sh"), buf.String())
131: 
132: 	return err
133: }
134: 
135: func (n *xrd) SaveConfig(_ context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {
136: 	err := clabnetconf.SaveRunningConfig(n.Cfg.LongName,
137: 		n.Cfg.Credentials.Username,
138: 		n.Cfg.Credentials.Password,
139: 		scrapliPlatformName,
140: 	)
141: 	if err != nil {
142: 		return nil, err
143: 	}
144: 
145: 	log.Infof("saved %s running configuration to startup configuration file\n", n.Cfg.ShortName)
146: 	return nil, nil
147: }
148: 
149: func (n *xrd) createXRDFiles(_ context.Context) error {
150: 	nodeCfg := n.Config()
151: 	// generate xr-storage directory
152: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "xr-storage"),
153: 		clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)
154: 	// generate first-boot config
155: 	cfg := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "first-boot.cfg")
156: 	nodeCfg.ResStartupConfig = cfg
157: 
158: 	mgmt_script_path := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "mgmt_intf_v6_addr.sh")
159: 
160: 	// generate script file
161: 	if !clabutils.FileExists(mgmt_script_path) {
162: 		clabutils.CreateFile(mgmt_script_path, "")
163: 		os.Chmod(mgmt_script_path, 0o775) // skipcq: GSC-G302
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	// set mgmt IPv4/IPv6 gateway as it is already known by now
167: 	// since the container network has been created before we launch nodes
168: 	// and mgmt gateway can be used in xrd.Cfg template to configure default route for mgmt
169: 	nodeCfg.MgmtIPv4Gateway = n.Runtime.Mgmt().IPv4Gw
170: 	nodeCfg.MgmtIPv6Gateway = n.Runtime.Mgmt().IPv6Gw
171: 
172: 	// use startup config file provided by a user
173: 	// make copy of template to prevent provided startup config from mutating shared package
174: 	// template value
175: 	currentCfgTemplate := cfgTemplate
176: 	if nodeCfg.StartupConfig != "" {
177: 		c, err := os.ReadFile(nodeCfg.StartupConfig)
178: 		if err != nil {
179: 			return err
180: 		}
181: 		currentCfgTemplate = string(c)
182: 	}
183: 
184: 	err := n.GenerateConfig(nodeCfg.ResStartupConfig, currentCfgTemplate)
185: 	if err != nil {
186: 		return err
187: 	}
188: 
189: 	return err
190: }
191: 
192: // genInterfacesEnv populates the content of a required env var that sets the interface mapping
193: // rules.
194: func (n *xrd) genInterfacesEnv() {
195: 	// xrd-control-plane variant needs XR_INTERFACE ENV var to be populated for all active interface
196: 	// here we take the number of links users set in the topology to get the right # of links
197: 	var interfaceEnvVar string
198: 
199: 	for _, ep := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {
200: 		// ifName is a linux interface name with dashes swapped for slashes to be used in the config
201: 		ifName := strings.ReplaceAll(ep.GetIfaceName(), "-", "/")
202: 		interfaceEnvVar += fmt.Sprintf("linux:%s,xr_name=%s;", ep.GetIfaceName(), ifName)
203: 	}
204: 
205: 	interfaceEnv := map[string]string{"XR_INTERFACES": interfaceEnvVar}
206: 
207: 	n.Cfg.Env = clabutils.MergeStringMaps(xrdEnv, interfaceEnv, n.Cfg.Env)
208: }
209: 
210: // CheckInterfaceName checks if a name of the interface referenced in the topology file correct.
211: func (n *xrd) CheckInterfaceName() error {
212: 	ifRe := regexp.MustCompile(`^Gi0-0-0-\d+$`)
213: 	for _, e := range n.Endpoints {
214: 		if !ifRe.MatchString(e.GetIfaceName()) {
215: 			return fmt.Errorf(
216: 				"cisco XRd interface name %q doesn't match the required pattern. XRd interfaces should be named as Gi0-0-0-X where X is the interface number",
217: 				e.GetIfaceName(),
218: 			)
219: 		}
220: 	}
221: 
222: 	return nil
223: }
224: 
225: type xrdScriptTmpl struct {
226: 	MgmtIPv6Addr      string
227: 	MgmtIPv6PrefixLen int
228: }

(End of file - total 228 lines)

Reasoning
Now check the base versions to confirm what changed (I have the diff already). Diff covers everything needed.

Next: check the cmd/save.go for docs accuracy (exit non-zero). Let me read cmd/save.go. Also check `docs/cmd/save.md` claims vs behavior — since `c.Save` now returns joined error, cobra RunE returns error → non-zero exit. Good.

Now let me think about whether `EndpointsWithParked` needs to be part of the Node interface — check if anything calls it via interface. grep for EndpointsWithParked across head.

Also check `slices` import in default_node.go — added. Check `errors` import in save.go — added.

Now the data race question — let me be very careful. In the goroutine closure:

```go
for _, node := range c.Nodes {
    errs = append(errs, nil)
    i := len(errs) - 1
    wg.Add(1)
    go func(node clabnodes.Node) {
        defer wg.Done()
        result, err := node.SaveConfig(ctx)
        if err != nil {
            errs[i] = fmt.Errorf(...)   // reads errs (variable), writes element
            return
        }
        ...
        errs[i] = fmt.Errorf(...)  // on copy failure
    }(node)
}
wg.Wait()
return errors.Join(errs...)
```

The closure captures `errs` and `i`. `i` is a new variable per iteration (declared inside loop body with `:=`), so each closure gets its own `i`. `errs` is shared. Concurrent write `errs = append(...)` by main vs read `errs` by goroutine → data race on the slice header variable. Also, if append reallocates, the goroutine reading the header after reallocation writes into the new array — correct value-wise. But if goroutine writes element into old array while main has already... hmm, actually there's another subtle hazard: if append reallocates, main copies old elements into new array. If a goroutine writes `errs[i]` on the old array concurrently with the copy, the copy could overwrite the goroutine's write with the stale nil, losing the error. Wait — the copy happens during append; the goroutine's write happens at any time. If goroutine writes before the copy starts, copy preserves it. If goroutine writes after copy but reads old header (stale pointer from before reallocation — it captured the header earlier? No, it reads the current header at execution time; if the append already completed, the header points to the new array, so write goes to the new array — fine). If goroutine reads header concurrently with header write (race), torn read possible. And a window: main is executing append: it has copied elements to new array but not yet published the new header; goroutine reads old header, writes old array element i → value lost when main returns errors.Join(errs...) which reads new array. That's a real lost-update hazard, not just theoretical. With cap preallocated = len(c.Nodes) (errs := make([]error, 0, len(c.Nodes))), append never reallocates! Cap is exactly len(c.Nodes), and we append exactly len(c.Nodes) elements. So no reallocation. But the header read/write race remains (header variable written by main each append even without reallocation — writes ptr/len/cap; len changes). Goroutine reading errs while main writes → race. Value-wise benign in practice (only len changes), but still a data race detectable by -race. So the finding: P2 data race on `errs` slice header; suggest preallocating `errs := make([]error, len(c.Nodes))` and indexing.

Wait, does the race detector actually flag reads/writes of slice headers? Yes, race detector instruments word-level accesses to the backing memory of variables; the slice header is three words; append writes len word; goroutine reads ptr word etc. Different words — TSan tracks per-memory-location (per 8-byte word?) race detection is per access range; it would flag overlapping access to the same word. Goroutine's element write `errs[i] = err` requires loading the `errs` pointer word (first word of header). Main's append writes the pointer word (same value, but still a write). Concurrent unsynchronized read/write of the same word → race reported. Yes.

Hmm — but actually, is the pointer word written by append? `errs = append(errs, nil)` — append writes the whole header (ptr, len, cap) even when no reallocation (len changes, and Go may write all three words). So yes, race.

Also, even simpler: the goroutine's read of `errs` may observe a partially updated header. In practice on amd64 it's word-atomic, so it reads either old or new consistent header. But it's still UB per memory model and race-detector-flagged. Good P2.

Now also check: is there any existing pattern in the repo for concurrent error collection? e.g., in `core/` there might be similar wg+errs patterns. Let me grep for `sync.WaitGroup` with `errs` in core. Not strictly necessary, but good for the "reuse existing pattern" rule. Let me check e.g. core/deploy or links deploy which do concurrent work.

Today is Nov 3, 2026 — "built with srl-labs/vrnetlab after November 2026" — fine as a claim, can't verify. Check whether there's any code in iol.go referencing port 5000/console — there's `base_port = 49000` for iouyap in GenInterfaceConfig; the telnet console 5000 is a vrnetlab image feature (qemu telnet console). The doc says console served on the node's management address — hmm, telnet to container name port 5000. That's plausible for vrnetlab images. But wait — does the IOL node expose port 5000? vrnetlab publishes telnet console ports? In containerlab, docker exec telnet localhost 5000? The doc example: `telnet <container-name/id> 5000` — connecting to the container by name requires the port to be exposed/published. vrnetlab typically exposes port 5000 (docker EXPOSE) but not published unless port config. `telnet <container-name> 5000` from the host works only if the port is published. From another container on the same docker network, it works via DNS name if the port is listening. Docker's embedded DNS resolves container names on user-defined networks; connecting to port 5000 on the container IP works if the process listens. vrnetlab's qemu console listens on 0.0.0.0:5000 inside container. So `telnet <container-name/id> 5000` from the host works if... resolving by container name from host doesn't work via docker DNS; but `docker inspect`... Actually `telnet <container-name> 5000` from host fails unless published. Hmm, but this is a docs example; the existing `docker exec -it <container-name/id> bash` uses the same placeholder. `docker exec` works with name from host obviously. For telnet from host, you'd need the mgmt IP or published port. The doc says "The console is served on the node's management address" — so you'd telnet to the management address, not the container name. The example `telnet <container-name/id> 5000` may be misleading: from the host, container name isn't resolvable (unless in /etc/hosts via clab's host entries? containerlab does add entries to /etc/hosts for node names! Yes — containerlab adds mgmt IP entries into /etc/hosts for lab nodes. So `telnet clab-<lab>-<node> 5000` from host resolves to the mgmt IP. That works.) OK, plausible.

3: ### Description
4: 
5: The `save` command perform configuration save for all the containers running in a lab.
6: 
7: When `save` fails, it will exit with a non-zero return code and reports each node that failed configuration save.
8: 
9: /// note | Clabernetes runtime
10: With `--runtime c9s`, `save` runs the kind-specific save command in each device
11: container. `save --copy` streams the resulting files back to the local
12: destination using the same copy layout as Docker labs. See
13: [Containerlab runtime](../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#save) for details.
14: ///
15: 
16: The exact command that performs configuration save depends on a given kind. The below table explains the method used for each kind:
17: 
18: | Kind               | Command                                     | Notes                                                   |
19: | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
20: | **Nokia SR Linux** | `sr_cli -d tools system configuration save` |                                                         |
21: | **Nokia SR OS**    |                                             | delivered via netconf RPC `copy-config running startup` |
22: | **Arista cEOS**    | `Cli -p 15 -c wr`                           |                                                         |
23: | **Juniper cSRX**   | `cli show conf`                             |                                                         |
24: | **Cisco IOL**      | `write memory`                              |                                                         |
25: 
26: --8<-- "docs/cmd/deploy.md:env-vars-flags"
27: 
28: ### Usage
29: 
30: `containerlab [global-flags] save [local-flags]`
31: 
32: ### Flags
33: 
34: #### topology | name
35: 
36: With the global `--topo | -t` flag a user sets the path to the topology definition file that will be used to spin up a lab.
37: 
38: When the topology path refers to a directory, containerlab will look for a file with `.clab.yml` extension in that directory and use it as a topology definition file.
39: 
40: When the topology file flag is omitted, containerlab will try to find the matching file name by looking at the current working directory.
41: 
42: If more than one file is found for directory-based path or when the flag is omitted entirely, containerlab will fail with an error.
43: 
44: Alternatively, use the global `--name` flag to derive the topology from a running lab. This requires the lab to be running and its containers to have the `topo-file` label; otherwise the save command will fail.
45: 
46: #### node-filter
47: 
48: The local `--node-filter` flag allows users to specify a subset of topology nodes targeted by `save` command. The value of this flag is a comma-separated list of node names as they appear in the topology.
49: 
50: When a subset of nodes is specified, containerlab will only attempt to save configuration on the selected nodes.
51: 
52: #### copy
53: 
54: The local `--copy` flag allows users to copy saved configuration files to a dedicated directory. The path is resolved relative to the current working directory unless an absolute path is provided.
55: 
56: When `--copy` is specified, containerlab copies the saved configuration file from its original location in the lab directory to the destination with a UTC timestamp embedded in the filename. A symlink with the original filename is created (or updated) to always point to the latest timestamped copy.
57: 
58: ```
59: <copy-path>/clab-<labname>/<node-name>/<config>-<YYMMDD_HHMMSS>.<ext>   # timestamped copy
60: <copy-path>/clab-<labname>/<node-name>/<config>.<ext>                    # symlink → latest
61: ```
62: 
63: The destination directory is created automatically if it does not exist. Running `save --copy` multiple times to the same directory preserves all previous saves, allowing easy rollback to an earlier configuration.
64: 
65: The exact file that is copied depends on the node kind and corresponds to the configuration file produced by the save operation:
66: 
67: | Kind               | Copied file                                  |
68: | ------------------ | -------------------------------------------- |
69: | **Nokia SR Linux** | `config/config.json`                         |
70: | **Nokia SR OS**    | `<slot>/config/cf3/config.cfg`               |
71: | **Arista cEOS**    | `flash/startup-config`                       |
72: | **Juniper cSRX**   | `config/juniper.conf`                        |
73: 
74: Node kinds that do not report a saved config path are silently skipped.
75: 
76: The `copy` flag opens up the path for saving the running configuration that is used for the startup config. By using the [magic variables](../manual/topo-def-file.md#magic-variables) in the topology file, you can point the startup config to the saved configuration without statically hardcoding the path.  
77: Consider the following lab:
78: 
79: ```yaml
80: name: mylab
81: topology:
82:   nodes:
83:     node1:
84:     node2:
85: ```
86: 
87: After running the lab, save the configuration to a directory:
88: 
89: ```bash
90: containerlab save --copy startup-configs -t mylab.clab.yml
91: ```
92: 
93: The saved configuration will be in the `startup-configs` directory which you can now specify as the startup config for the nodes of the same topology using `__clabLabName__` and `__clabNodeName__` magic variables:
94: 
95: ```yaml
96: name: mylab
97: topology:
98:   nodes:
99:     node1:
100:       startup-config: startup-configs/__clabLabName__/__clabNodeName__/config.json
101:     node2:
102:       startup-config: startup-configs/__clabLabName__/__clabNodeName__/config.json
103: ```
104: 
105: ### Examples
106: 
107: #### Save the configuration of the containers in a specific lab
108: 
109: Save the configuration of the containers running in lab named srl02
110: 
111: ```bash
112: ❯ containerlab save --name srl02
113: INFO[0001] clab-srl02-srl1: stdout: /system:
114:     Generated checkpoint '/etc/opt/srlinux/checkpoint/checkpoint-0.json' with name 'checkpoint-2020-11-18T09:00:54.998Z' and comment ''
115: 
116: INFO[0002] clab-srl02-srl2: stdout: /system:
117:     Generated checkpoint '/etc/opt/srlinux/checkpoint/checkpoint-0.json' with name 'checkpoint-2020-11-18T09:00:56.444Z' and comment ''
118: ```
119: 
120: #### Save and copy configs to a reusable directory
121: 
122: ```bash
123: ❯ containerlab save -t srl02.clab.yml --copy ./startup-configs
124: ```
125: 
126: This creates timestamped copies with symlinks pointing to the latest save:
127: 
128: ```
129: ./startup-configs/clab-srl02/srl1/config-260207_091500.json   # timestamped copy
130: ./startup-configs/clab-srl02/srl1/config.json                 # symlink → config-260207_091500.json
131: ./startup-configs/clab-srl02/srl2/config-260207_091500.json
132: ./startup-configs/clab-srl02/srl2/config.json
133: ```
134: 
135: Running the same command again creates new timestamped files and updates the symlinks, while the previous saves remain in the directory.

(End of file - total 135 lines)
</content>

30: - 768Mb of RAM.
31: - 1Mb of disk space for the NVRAM (where configuration is saved).
32: 
33: Using [KSM](../vrnetlab.md#memory-optimization) you can achieve a higher density of IOL nodes per GB of RAM.
34: 
35: ## Managing -{{ kind_display_name }}- nodes
36: 
37: You can manage the -{{ kind_display_name }}- with containerlab via the following interfaces:
38: 
39: /// tab | CLI
40: to connect to the -{{ kind_short_display_name }}- CLI
41: 
42: ```bash
43: ssh admin@<container-name/id>
44: ```
45: 
46: ///
47: /// tab | bash
48: to connect to a `bash` shell of a running -{{ kind_short_display_name }}- container:
49: 
50: ```bash
51: docker exec -it <container-name/id> bash
52: ```
53: 
54: ///
55: /// tab | Telnet
56: to connect to the -{{ kind_short_display_name }}- console via telnet
57: 
58: ```bash
59: telnet <container-name/id> 5000
60: ```
61: 
62: The console is served on the node's management address, so it stays reachable when the IOS management configuration is broken. After the node boots, press Enter first: IOS ignores console input until it receives a lone Enter.
63: 
64: /// warning | Console requires a recent image
65: The telnet console requires an -{{ kind_short_display_name }}- image built with srl-labs/vrnetlab after November 2026. Images built from an older vrnetlab revision do not serve a console on port 5000.
66: ///
67: 
68: ///
69: 
70: /// note
71: Default credentials: `admin:admin`
72: ///
73: 
74: ## Interface naming
75: 
76: You can use [interfaces names](../topo-def-file.md#interface-naming) in the topology file like they appear in the -{{ kind_display_name }}- CLI.
77: 
78: The interface naming convention is: `Ethernet0/X` (or `e0/X`), where `X` is the port number.
79: 
80: With the default `Ethernet0/0` management interface:
81: 
82: - `e0/1` - First data-plane interface available
83: - `e0/2` - Second data-plane interface, and so on...
84: 
85: Keep in mind IOL defines interfaces in groups of 4. Every four interfaces (zero-indexed), the slot index increments by one.
86: 
87: - `e0/3` - Third data-plane interface
88: - `e1/0` - Fourth data-plane interface
89: - `e1/1` - Fifth data-plane interface
90: 
91: The example ports above would be mapped to the following Linux interfaces inside the container running -{{ kind_display_name }}-:
92: 
93: - `eth0` - management interface connected to the containerlab management network. Mapped to `Ethernet0/0`.
94: - `eth1` - First data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet0/1` interface.
95: - `eth2` - Second data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet0/2` interface
96: - `eth3` - Third data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet0/3` interface
97: - `eth4` - Fourth data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet1/0` interface
98: - `eth5` - Fifth data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet1/1` interface and so on...
99: 
100: When containerlab launches -{{ kind_display_name }}-, the `Ethernet0/0` interface of the container gets assigned management IPv4 and IPv6 addresses from docker. The `Ethernet0/0` interface is in it's own management VRF so that configuration in the global context will not affect the management interface.
101: 
102: Interfaces can be defined in a non-contiguous manner in your topology file. See the example below.
103: 
104: ```yaml
105: name: my-iol-lab
106: topology:
107:   nodes:
108:     iol-1:
109:       kind: cisco_iol

18: func NewParkingNode(containerName, nsPath string) *ParkingNode {
19: 	return &ParkingNode{
20: 		GenericLinkNode: GenericLinkNode{
21: 			shortname: clabutils.ParkingNetnsName(containerName),
22: 			endpoints: []Endpoint{},
23: 			nspath:    nsPath,

25: 		containerName: containerName,

29: func (p *ParkingNode) RepointSymlink() error {
30: 	return clabutils.LinkContainerNS(p.nspath, p.containerName)
31: }
32: 
33: func (p *ParkingNode) CaptureFrom(ctx context.Context, src Node) error {
34: 	endpoints, err := p.captureCandidates(ctx, src)
35: 	if err != nil {
36: 		return err
37: 	}
38: 
39: 	moved := make([]Endpoint, 0, len(endpoints))
40: 	for _, ep := range endpoints {
41: 		if err := moveEndpoint(ctx, ep, p); err != nil {
42: 			for i := len(moved) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
43: 				if err := moveEndpoint(ctx, moved[i], src); err == nil {
44: 					_ = moved[i].Activate(ctx)
45: 				}
46: 			}
47: 			return fmt.Errorf(
48: 				"failed to park interface %q for node %q: %w",
49: 				ep.GetIfaceName(),
50: 				src.GetShortName(),
51: 				err,
52: 			)
53: 		}
54: 		moved = append(moved, ep)
55: 	}
56: 
57: 	return nil
58: }
59: 
60: func (p *ParkingNode) RestoreTo(ctx context.Context, dst Node) ([]Endpoint, error) {
61: 	if err := p.DiscoverOwnedEndpoints(ctx, dst); err != nil {
62: 		return nil, err
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	endpoints := append([]Endpoint(nil), p.GetEndpoints()...)
66: 	moved := make([]Endpoint, 0, len(endpoints))
67: 
68: 	for _, ep := range endpoints {
69: 		if err := moveEndpoint(ctx, ep, dst); err != nil {
70: 			for i := len(moved) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
71: 				_ = moveEndpoint(ctx, moved[i], p)
72: 			}
73: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf(
74: 				"failed to restore interface %q for node %q: %w",
75: 				ep.GetIfaceName(),
76: 				dst.GetShortName(),
77: 				err,
78: 			)
79: 		}
80: 		if err := ep.Activate(ctx); err != nil {
81: 			_ = moveEndpoint(ctx, ep, p)
82: 			for i := len(moved) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
83: 				_ = moveEndpoint(ctx, moved[i], p)
84: 			}
85: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf(
86: 				"failed to activate interface %q for node %q: %w",
87: 				ep.GetIfaceName(),
88: 				dst.GetShortName(),
89: 				err,
90: 			)
91: 		}
92: 		moved = append(moved, ep)
93: 	}
94: 
95: 	return moved, nil
96: }
97: 
98: func (p *ParkingNode) DiscoverOwnedEndpoints(ctx context.Context, original Node) error {
99: 	ifaceNames, err := listOwnedInterfaceNames(
100: 		ctx,
101: 		p,
102: 		trackedIfaceNames(original.GetEndpoints(), p.GetEndpoints()),
103: 	)
104: 	if err != nil {
105: 		return err
106: 	}
107: 
108: 	discovered := make(map[string]struct{}, len(ifaceNames))
109: 	for _, ifaceName := range ifaceNames {
110: 		discovered[ifaceName] = struct{}{}
111: 	}
112: 
113: 	for _, ep := range append([]Endpoint(nil), p.GetEndpoints()...) {
114: 		if _, exists := discovered[ep.GetIfaceName()]; exists {
115: 			continue
116: 		}
117: 
118: 		if err := p.ReleaseEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
119: 			return err
120: 		}
121: 		if ep.IsRuntimeDiscovered() {
122: 			continue
123: 		}
124: 
125: 		ep.SetNode(original)
126: 		if err := original.AdoptEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
127: 			return err
128: 		}
129: 	}
130: 
131: 	known := make(map[string]Endpoint, len(original.GetEndpoints())+len(p.GetEndpoints()))
132: 	for _, ep := range append([]Endpoint(nil), original.GetEndpoints()...) {
133: 		known[ep.GetIfaceName()] = ep
134: 	}
135: 	for _, ep := range append([]Endpoint(nil), p.GetEndpoints()...) {
136: 		known[ep.GetIfaceName()] = ep
137: 	}
138: 
139: 	for _, ifaceName := range ifaceNames {
140: 		if ep, ok := known[ifaceName]; ok {
141: 			if ep.GetNode() == Node(p) {
142: 				continue
143: 			}
144: 
145: 			if err := ep.GetNode().ReleaseEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
146: 				return err
147: 			}
148: 			ep.SetNode(p)
149: 			if err := p.AdoptEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
150: 				return err
151: 			}
152: 			continue
153: 		}
154: 
155: 		runtimeEp := NewRuntimeEndpoint(p, ifaceName)
156: 		if err := p.AdoptEndpoint(runtimeEp); err != nil {
157: 			return err
158: 		}
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	return nil
162: }
163: 
164: func (p *ParkingNode) captureCandidates(
165: 	ctx context.Context,
166: 	src Node,
167: ) ([]Endpoint, error) {
168: 	trackedEndpoints := src.GetEndpoints()
169: 	tracked := make(map[string]Endpoint, len(trackedEndpoints))
170: 	for _, ep := range trackedEndpoints {
171: 		tracked[ep.GetIfaceName()] = ep
172: 	}
173: 	presentIfaceNames, err := listOwnedInterfaceNames(ctx, src, trackedIfaceNames(trackedEndpoints))
174: 	if err != nil {
175: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
176: 			"failed to discover runtime interfaces for node %q: %w",
177: 			src.GetShortName(),
178: 			err,
179: 		)
180: 	}
181: 
182: 	endpoints := make([]Endpoint, 0, len(presentIfaceNames))
183: 	for _, ifaceName := range presentIfaceNames {
184: 		ep, ok := tracked[ifaceName]
185: 		if !ok {
186: 			ep = NewRuntimeEndpoint(src, ifaceName)
187: 			if err := src.AdoptEndpoint(ep); err != nil {
188: 				return nil, err
189: 			}
190: 		}
191: 
192: 		endpoints = append(endpoints, ep)
193: 	}
194: 
195: 	return endpoints, nil
196: }
197: 
198: func trackedIfaceNames(endpointSets ...[]Endpoint) map[string]struct{} {
199: 	ifaceNames := map[string]struct{}{}
200: 
201: 	for _, endpoints := range endpointSets {
202: 		for _, ep := range endpoints {
203: 			ifaceNames[ep.GetIfaceName()] = struct{}{}
204: 		}
205: 	}
206: 
207: 	return ifaceNames
208: }
209: 
210: func listOwnedInterfaceNames(
211: 	ctx context.Context,
212: 	node Node,
213: 	knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{},
214: ) ([]string, error) {
215: 	var ifaces []string
216: 
217: 	err := node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(_ ns.NetNS) error {
218: 		links, err := netlink.LinkList()
219: 		if err != nil {
220: 			return err
221: 		}
222: 
223: 		for _, link := range links {
224: 			if !isOwnedInterface(link, knownIfaceNames) {
225: 				continue
226: 			}
227: 
228: 			ifaces = append(ifaces, link.Attrs().Name)
229: 		}
230: 
231: 		return nil
232: 	})
233: 	if err != nil {
234: 		return nil, err
235: 	}
236: 
237: 	slices.Sort(ifaces)
238: 
239: 	return ifaces, nil
240: }
241: 
242: func isOwnedInterface(link netlink.Link, knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{}) bool {
243: 	name := link.Attrs().Name
244: 	switch name {
245: 	case "lo", "eth0":
246: 		return false
247: 	}
248: 
249: 	if _, known := knownIfaceNames[name]; !known && !hasOwnershipAltName(link) {
250: 		return false
251: 	}
252: 
253: 	return true
254: }
255: 
256: func (*ParkingNode) GetLinkEndpointType() LinkEndpointType {
257: 	return LinkEndpointTypeVeth
258: }

Reasoning
ParkingNode embeds GenericLinkNode, which has GetEndpoints and AddEndpoint presumably. Good.

Now, an important consideration: in the test, parked endpoints are added via `d.parkingNode.AddEndpoint(veth(d.parkingNode, iface))` — this assigns the endpoint's node to the parking node. In real flow, parked endpoints keep their node set to... `moveEndpoint` and `CaptureFrom` — endpoints moved to parking; `ep.SetNode(p)`? In captureCandidates, ep is from src (node itself); after CaptureFrom the endpoint object's node may still be the original node but tracked in p's endpoints? Actually `moveEndpoint(ctx, ep, p)` moves the veth; does it update ep.SetNode? Let me not go too deep. The point: `EndpointsWithParked` returns `d.parkingNode.GetEndpoints()` — these are endpoint objects possibly still pointing at the original node as their node; that only matters for GetIfaceName which is what the callers use. Fine.

Now, when is `Deploy` called while parkingNode != nil? In the reconcile/redeploy flow. Check core/deploy.go or topology_reconcile for the sequence: ParkEndpoints happens on destroy/redeploy of a running node? Let me check where ParkEndpoints is called.

Actually the scenario: `clab redeploy` or `deploy --recreate`? Let's grep ParkEndpoints/RestoreEndpoints callers.

Reasoning
So `boot_config.txt` is written by `GenBootConfig` in PostDeploy, and mounted as `/iol/config.txt`. The change in `CreateIOLFiles` (PreDeploy) only avoids pre-truncating it — PostDeploy always regenerates via GenBootConfig. So the behavior change: on `redeploy` of an IOL node (apply recreate flow where PreDeploy runs again before PostDeploy), previously boot_config.txt got truncated (empty file) between park and container recreate — wait, actually the order: PreDeploy (truncate to empty) → create/start container → PostDeploy (GenBootConfig writes full config). During the window, the container is running with an empty config mounted... but actually the container reads config.txt at boot; truncation before boot then regeneration after boot means the container may boot with empty config? Hmm, actually PostDeploy runs after container start. So previously: PreDeploy truncates boot_config.txt (mounted /iol/config.txt), container starts, reads empty config at boot → then PostDeploy regenerates config (but the container already booted). That seems like the pre-existing bug that this fix addresses: preserving boot_config.txt when it exists means the recreated container boots with the previous boot config. That's the intent (redeploy preserves config). Good change, no issue.

Wait, but there's a subtlety: on first boot, nvram doesn't exist → firstBoot = true → boot with empty boot_config.txt is fine. On recreate (not first boot), preserve boot_config.txt. Makes sense.

Now, what about `n.firstBoot` — set in CreateIOLFiles; with boot_config preserved, firstBoot remains false. Consistent.

Next, examine the diff's doc claim about "press Enter first: IOS ignores console input until it receives a lone Enter" — unverifiable, fine.

/// note
...
```

Hmm — the warning admonition is nested inside the Telnet tab and closed with `///`, then the tab is closed with `///`. That's correct material-markdown nesting? Actually in Material for MkDocs tab syntax, nested admonitions inside tabs use `///` blocks... The structure:

```
/// tab | Telnet
text

```bash
...
```

text...

/// warning | Console requires a recent image
The telnet console requires...
///

///
```

Yes, nested `/// warning` inside `/// tab` closed before closing tab. That's valid. The bash tab in base is closed with `///` before the note. Fine.

Now — anything else? Let me review the save_test.go for a subtle issue: the mock `SaveConfig` returns `(nil, err)`. In the failing case, `result` is nil and err != nil → returns early. OK. In success case `result` nil and err nil, copyDst == "" (default NewSaveOptions) → return. OK.

Also `ctrl := gomock.NewController(t)` — fine (auto-cleanup with t).

One test-related check: `TestSaveReturnsNodeErrors` — with 3 nodes and failing r2 only, the loop's goroutine for r2 writes errs[i]. Race exists in test but no -race in CI apparently. Fine.

Now think about whether there might be another race: `errs` preallocated with cap == len(c.Nodes), so appends never reallocate. The remaining race is on the header variable. Real but subtle. Should I report it as P2? The pattern is a genuine Go data race detectable by `-race` and theoretically lossy. Let me double-check the Go semantics once more: the goroutine closure references `errs` — the closure captures the variable by reference. `errs[i] = err` compiles to: load slice header of errs (at least the data pointer), compute addr, store. Main concurrently executes `errs = append(errs, nil)` which stores the header. Unsynced → data race. Yes.

Actually wait — one more consideration: does the compiler capture `errs` by reference in the closure? The closure doesn't assign errs, only reads it. Go closures capture variables by reference (they share the same variable). Yes, since `errs` is assigned after the closure body is defined (in later loop iterations), the compiler must capture by reference.

So finding 1: P2 data race on `errs` slice header in `CLab.Save`. Fix: preallocate `errs := make([]error, len(c.Nodes))` and assign by index without appending in the loop (or collect nodes into a slice first). This removes the concurrent header read/write. Suggest.

Hmm, but wait — is there really a concurrent read of `errs` in the goroutine? `errs[i] = ...` — the address computation needs the data pointer from the header. But could the compiler hoist/copy the header value into the goroutine at `go` statement time? The go statement evaluates the closure and its captured variables... For captured variables, the closure environment holds a pointer to the variable (boxing). So at execution time, it dereferences to the current header. So yes, read at execution time.

However, one could argue the race detector might not flag because the write in main (append) and the read in goroutine (header load) — TSan would flag if they overlap in the vector-clock sense without synchronization. There's no sync edge between main's 2nd iteration append and goroutine 1's execution... Actually wait, there IS no edge — wg.Add(1) before go statement is done by main; the goroutine's start happens with the release from `go` itself: the `go` statement creates a happens-before edge between the goroutine's start and everything before it in main (before the go statement). But main's *subsequent* iterations (after the go statement) have no edge to the goroutine. So the 2nd append (after go stmt) is unsynchronized with goroutine 1's reads. Race. Confirmed.

Trigger: `clab save` on a lab where a node's `SaveConfig` fails (or copy fails) while `CLab.Save` is still spawning goroutines for remaining nodes — e.g., any multi-node lab with a failed node. Impact: undefined behavior per Go memory model; `go test -race` failures; theoretically lost or duplicated error reports (e.g., torn header read could make `errs[i]` write go to a stale/wrong location, losing an error → `save` could exit 0 despite failures, breaking the newly documented non-zero-exit contract).

Wait, can the lost error actually happen? Scenario: cap == len(c.Nodes) exactly, so no reallocation. Header pointer stays same; only len changes. Goroutine reads header (ptr same, len whatever) and writes element i (i < len at spawn time... the element index i is valid in the array regardless of len since cap covers it). Write goes to correct array slot. So value-wise correct in practice. Torn read of header — on amd64, individual word loads are atomic; header fields are loaded separately; ptr is stable. So practically benign, but still a race that -race flags and that violates the memory model. Also on weaker memory models (arm64), the read of len vs write of len could produce stale len — but errs[i] only needs ptr and i; i is from the captured variable, not the header. So element address computation uses ptr (stable) and i. So the only header read that matters is ptr. ptr never changes (no realloc). So value-correct in practice; race only per memory model / race detector. Hmm — but TSan considers it a race regardless.

Given impact is mostly `-race` CI noise and theoretical UB, P2 seems right per "correctness/reliability defect". Actually maybe P3? The definition: P2 correctness/reliability defect; P3 minor defect. A data race is a correctness defect per Go standards; it's exactly the kind of thing `go test -race`/golangci-lint (go race detector in CI) would flag. Also, maintainers may run tests with -race locally. I'll go P2.

Hmm, but let me reconsider — is there any real risk of error loss? No (ptr stable). Risk: race-detector failure in CI if -race enabled (didn't find -race in CI), plus memory-model violation. I'll set P2 with honest framing: flagged by race detector, benign in current form only because capacity is preallocated; still should use the preallocated-index pattern.

96: // copySavedConfig copies the saved configuration file from the path in the SaveConfigResult
97: // to the user-specified destination directory with a timestamp in the filename.

111: 	if result == nil || result.ConfigPath == "" {

138: 	linkPath := filepath.Join(nodeDstDir, fileName)
139: 	_ = os.Remove(linkPath)
140: 	if err := os.Symlink(tsFileName, linkPath); err != nil {
141: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create symlink %q -> %q: %w", linkPath, tsFileName, err)

154: // timestampedFileName inserts a timestamp before the file extension.
155: // e.g. "config.json" with ts 2026-02-06 23:59:00 UTC -> "config-260206_235900.json".
156: // Files without an extension get the timestamp appended: "startup-config" ->

158: func timestampedFileName(name string, t time.Time) string {
